# Bug register

_Generated from `docs/bugs/bugs.json` with AmiWind v0.0.50._

The single list of every AmiWind bug and its current status. How tracking
works, the report template and the rules: [bug tracking](bugs/README.md).
Dated events are in the [journal](BUG_JOURNAL.md); full reports are in
[docs/bugs](bugs/README.md). CHIM engine bugs by part, with the build each was found
in: [CHIM Engine tracker](bugs/CHIM_TRACKER.md).

## Fix index

Generated from [`docs/bugs/bugs.json`](bugs/bugs.json): do not edit this table
by hand. Change the JSON (or use `tools/bug_register.py add` / `set`), then run
`tools/bug_register.py render`. A test fails if the two disagree.

`fixed` means a correction shipped in the named version with supporting
verification; candidates and mitigations stay `open`. `closed` means not a bug,
a duplicate or superseded (the status says which).

<!-- BEGIN GENERATED BUG REGISTER: edit docs/bugs/bugs.json, then run tools/bug_register.py render -->

1117 bugs: 544 open, 486 fixed, 87 closed.

### Persisting, not resolved

Bugs reported two or more times. Most reports first. Each has its report lines on its page.

- [BUILD-CHIM-CELLS-PRIVATE-LIST-47](bugs/BUILD-CHIM-CELLS-PRIVATE-LIST-47.md): The releases' CHIM cell list was not in the source tree; public builds had no CHIM exterior cells (reported 11 times, open)
- [PLAYER-TORCH-THIRDPERSON-38](bugs/PLAYER-TORCH-THIRDPERSON-38.md): Player torch and holding pose disappear in third person while light remains (reported 9 times, open)
- [BUILD-IMAGE-UNDERUSED-32](bugs/BUILD-IMAGE-UNDERUSED-32.md): Parts of the image step still run serially or leave most CPUs idle (reported 8 times, fixed)
- [BUILD-REUSE-KEYS-TOO-BROAD-35](bugs/BUILD-REUSE-KEYS-TOO-BROAD-35.md): A whole build reused no stage, and the cost was not visible before the build started (reported 8 times, open)
- [MESH-LOD-TORN-MESHES-33](bugs/MESH-LOD-TORN-MESHES-33.md): Island-wide seam audit: 49 reduced meshes tear their seams (town flora, rocks, the arrival ship) (reported 8 times, open)
- [CHIM-AZURA-FLOATING-FLORA-43](bugs/CHIM-AZURA-FLOATING-FLORA-43.md): Plant or tree pieces hang in the air at Azura's Coast (cell 16,7) (reported 7 times, open)
- [BUILD-CHIM-STAGE-SERIAL-TAIL-36](bugs/BUILD-CHIM-STAGE-SERIAL-TAIL-36.md): After the parallel world build the chim stage ran its validation, heap gate and zone walk on one core (about 10 minutes of the 270-frame coast build) (reported 6 times, open)

- [BUILD-PIPELINE-UNDERUTILIZATION-36](bugs/BUILD-PIPELINE-UNDERUTILIZATION-36.md): Umbrella: several builder phases run on one core while the rest of the machine idles, and some do work the build no longer needs (reported 5 times, open)

| AUDIO-ENTER-29 | Sub-second soundtrack cut on Enter to follow the guard | medium | v0.0.29-rc1 | open | - | - | Still present in v0.0.30-rc1 (maintainer, WinUAE, 7 October 2026): the music pauses for a split second on Enter to follow the guard in the prison ship. Maintainer reports this worked in earlier versions, so it is a regression from a music-servicing (polling) change; bisect against older builds. Appearance-entry acceptance is split into AUDIO-APPEARANCE-29; heavy-load crackling into AUDIO-LOAD-29. Cause open. |
| AUDIO-LOAD-29 | OST crackles under heavy loading | high | v0.0.29-rc1 | open | - | - | Still present in v0.0.30-rc1 (maintainer, WinUAE, 7 October 2026): heavy music crackling going from the ship's hull to the deck, and small crackles entering the Census and Excise Office. Control cases from the same playtest, both without disk loads: no crackle while choosing character attributes or reading the release papers in the Census office. Points at the mixer starving during blocking loads rather than at the music path. Project priority: the soundtrack must stay clean through loads. Related WIN-05 history retained; cause and independent reproduction pending. The maintainer, 24 October 2026: the crackle depends on the emulator: FS-UAE barely crackles during the loads, WinUAE much more. Fits the mixer starving during a blocking load: the length of the load (the emulated disk timing) and the emulator s sound buffer decide how much of the gap is heard; real hardware is likely closer to WinUAE. Source (triage of 24 October 2026): no music or mixer update between the map and model reads (common.c, model.c, sv_main.c), unlike aw_head.c. Repair (v0.0.51, fixable now): service the music between chunks of those reads; verify on WinUAE through the opening (hull to deck, the census office). |

| [BUILD-CHIM-REGION-FRAMES-TAIL-43](bugs/BUILD-CHIM-REGION-FRAMES-TAIL-43.md) | The CHIM region-frame step runs its last frames on one worker while the other cores sit idle | medium | v0.0.43-dev3 (2026_10_18_v0.0.43-dev3_full-batch5_ef7e464) | open | - | - | Cause read 18 October 2026: prepare_areas_parallel passed the frames to ordered_map without a cost, so heavy frames late in the list started last and ran alone; the placement layouts of all 1,351 frames were made on one core (3 min 42 s) before the visibility rows started. Repair on branch v0.0.43-scheduler: region frames largest first (cost history 'chim-region-frames', else the references in their source cells), ordered_map history=/key= (largest first whenever a history exists), cost= on completed_map and governed_map, the image step's region and town frame maps by their history, layouts made when the visibility rows ask for them (overlapping the rows), and a sweep test that every pool caller gives a cost or a recorded reason. Verification: the next full build. |

| CHIM-CAVE21-NONCONVEX-FACE-48 | The cave piece shared by in_moldcave_21, in_mudcave_21, in_pycave_21 and in_lavacave_21 has a non-convex face, so its collision falls back to the piece chain in every room that uses it | medium | v0.0.48-dev2 (2026_10_23_v0.0.48-rc1_rc1_18a24ead) | open | - | - | Open (24 October 2026): seen by the maintainer in the v0.0.48-rc1 build log and present since dev2 try 13. All four meshes are one geometry packet (6db52335d50b7e51) with different textures; the collision compiler refuses it every time with "CheckFace: Found a non-convex face (error size 0.802837, point: -64.906250 67.406248 -45.484377)", also after the coplanar-facet merge retry, and the builder falls back to the piece chain (19 times in dev2 try 14, 11 so far in rc1). The rooms still build and pass their heap gates; the cost is larger collision data per room and a longer room stage. Single meshes do the same less often (dev2 try 14: in_ar_shellbottom_01, in_ar_shelltop_01, in_ci_01, in_v_arena_01; rc1: ex_vivec_c_02). Fix: weld or split the one bad face of that packet before the union (a planar tolerance for faces off by under a unit), so the union compiles. |

| CHIM-ROOM-16MB-REFUSAL-48 | On 16 MB of Fast RAM (or with Extra RAM off) a CHIM room larger than the zone ended the game with a host error at its door | high | v0.0.48-dev2 | open | - | - | Fixed in source on the v0.0.48 line (23 October 2026), in-game check pending. The maintainer: a loading-screen style background with one box in the original UI style saying "Sorry, but this interior cannot be run with 16MB of Fast RAM." then "Press ENTER to return."; ENTER returns the player to the spot outside the door, never stranded; decided at build time: each room that needs 32 MB is marked, and the engine checks the mark and the Fast RAM it found before loading anything; at 32 MB and more the door loads the room as usual. Done: the image step room heap gate marks such room maps ("_chim_room_fast_mb" "32" in the worldspawn; over the 16 MB zone alone and within the measured 32 MB room model of the door graph, CHIM-ROOM-ESTIMATE-48: the zone and the automatic cache bank, the largest model within one block; the same thresholds as the room plan's zone_over_16mb note) and fails only a room over that; the door (aw_scene.c AW_SceneRoomRefuse) reads the mark before the door sound or the map command, plays the locked sound, shows the screen on the loading art (aw_ui.c AW_UIRoomRefused) and returns on ENTER; Extra RAM off counts as the 16 MB baseline. The build prints how many rooms it marked. |

| [CHIM-TERRAIN-TEXTURE-BLOCKY-43](bugs/CHIM-TERRAIN-TEXTURE-BLOCKY-43.md) | Roads and paths on the ground look blocky: each texture square is drawn with hard edges instead of blending into its neighbours | low | v0.0.43-dev2 (2026_10_18_v0.0.43-dev2_full-batch3_b55a5ce) | open | - | - | Reported 18 October 2026 by the maintainer ('the road paths look like shit atm'; every path of the game): a path breaks into separate squares instead of reading as one track. Morrowind's land texture layer is 16 x 16 squares per cell and blends neighbouring squares; ours draws one texture per square with hard edges. Planned A/B, first option the maintainer's (20:04): (d) DIAGONAL SPLITS: where a square's texture meets a different texture on two adjacent sides (a corner of a stair-step edge), split that square along its diagonal into two triangles, each with one texture, so stair-steps become 45-degree edges (marching-squares over the texture grid; about one extra face per split square, no new textures). Then, if needed: (a) baked transition textures for the edge pairs that occur, (b) a dithered edge band baked into the surfaces, (c) finer squares along roads. Measured in faces, memory and frame time; OpenMW at the same pose. |

| [CHIM-VIVEC-DOCK-WALKWAY-43](bugs/CHIM-VIVEC-DOCK-WALKWAY-43.md) | The player cannot climb the walkway at the Vivec Hlaalu canton dock and gets stuck | medium | v0.0.43-dev2 (2026_10_18_v0.0.43-dev2_full-batch3_b55a5ce) | open | - | - | Cause measured 18 October 2026: both spots (2,-11 ref 278930; 4,-11 ref 278913) are ex_v_dock_01. mesh_geometry.collision_parts built each convex collision piece from its extreme points along 14 directions; on the dock deck (level, then sloped) that hull lies up to 19.4 units under the planks the authored collision follows (dev2 frame at the first spot: collision 143.8, planks 160.8). The dev3 stair fix does not change it. Island-wide: 535 exterior meshes have walkable collision more than a step over their pieces. Fix (branch v0.0.43-dockwalk): the 484 measured meshes of config/collision-hull-surface.json keep all points of a piece whose reduced hull leaves its source more than 0.5 units outside (68,666 placements; hull bytes 1.06 to 1.63 MB stored once; at most +110 KB at a frame heap peak, no frame over budget on the dev2 world); a collision surface gate checks every CHIM mesh. Branch brought up to the v0.0.45 dev line 20 October 2026; verification waits for a build. |

| [CHIM-ZONE-BUDGET-33](bugs/CHIM-ZONE-BUDGET-33.md) | The engine's default CHIM zone does not hold the active ring of Seyda Neen or of Balmora's south-west corner | medium | v0.0.33-dev | open | - | - | Open: the engine default is chim_zone_kib 6,864 with a 2 x 384 KiB frame-world pool (0d8bf4f, shipped in v0.0.33): 6,242,304 bytes of chunk room, which the heap gate reads from the engine source (engine_limits.chim_memory); the interim 7,680 KiB builder budget is gone. Open: a zone each world states itself (proposed). PERSISTING in v0.0.49 and v0.0.50 (24 October 2026): the boot smokes log CHIM chunk zone-full retries in Balmora (the zone locked 7,021,952 of 7,028,736 bytes; chunks 394, 401, 360 and others retried): v0.0.49 final at 32 MB 234 lines, v0.0.50 final 306 at 32 MB and 511 at 16 MB. Every smoke passed (the chunks load on a retry). On the v0.0.51 list (persisting bugs, item 0.4). The zone does not grow with Fast RAM: chim_zone_kib stays 6,864 KiB (engine/aga/src/chim/chim_world.c) at 32 MB as at 16 MB, so the retries happen at both (the maintainer asked, 24 October 2026); the RAM past 16 MB goes to the CHIM cache bank, not the zone. Repair (v0.0.51): size the zone from the Fast RAM found at start, like the heap and the cache bank, and gate the build on Balmora loading with no zone-full retry at 32 MB. Where it bites: the old town frames. Seyda Neen, Balmora and Vivec were built as one big frame each, before the one-cell region frames, so a whole town s chunks share the one fixed zone (the maintainer, 24 October 2026: the original cities might have this same fixed setting problem). The retries in the v0.0.49 and v0.0.50 smokes are all in Balmora. To check for all three in v0.0.51 at 16 and 32 MB; the old town frames as ordinary region frames (v0.0.51 list) would end it for good. |

| IMAGE-FORMATS-CHIM-ROOMS-48 | The payload preflight stops a CHIM rooms build: the image step does not know the room store and room record formats | high | v0.0.48-dev2 (2026_10_22_v0.0.48-dev2_miniwind-balmora-rooms-chim_d3e50c1e) | open | - | - | Repaired on the v0.0.48 interiors branch (22 October 2026): the room store, room records and room maps (maps/<room>.chim, a Quake BSP under its own name) pass the image step; seen twice (store files first, then room maps); tests added. |
| [IMAGE-LAYOUT-PLAN-ACTOR-LIBRARY-48](bugs/IMAGE-LAYOUT-PLAN-ACTOR-LIBRARY-48.md) | The image step stopped at its disk layout check: the layout plan left out the actor library it installs (+145 MB on a MiniWind boot partition) | high | v0.0.48-dev2 (2026_10_22_v0.0.48-dev2_miniwind-balmora-rooms-bsp_dd768704) | open | - | - | PERSISTING in v0.0.48-rc1 (24 October 2026), worked around for v0.0.48: rc1-try3 stopped 43 minutes into the image step: "The staged payload no longer matches the layout plan: DW0 holds other maps than planned (211 differ ...); DW0 holds boot units not planned there (30 ...)". The payload preflight planned 2.45 GB of boot payload on 2 partitions; the CHIM room actor bake then wrote its fitted actor models inside the step and the payload reached 3.01 GB, 3 partitions (dev2 grew 2.40 -> 2.71 GB and stayed on 2). rc1 actor models are about 6-8 % bigger each than dev2 (room batches: 6,844 models, 2,727 MB against 6,938, 2,593 MB) and about 2,000 more distinct models reach the image. Worked around: a payload that only grew is laid out from the staged files (layout_plan.follows_or_grew); require_minimal and the headroom gate still decide. The real fix (v0.0.49, the maintainer: "shouldn't we sort of figure out the size from what we have created?"): the room actor bake becomes its own cached stage before the image, so the plan is computed from files that exist. |

| [ROOM-RESIDENT-MODELS-SIZE-48](bugs/ROOM-RESIDENT-MODELS-SIZE-48.md) | The batch rooms' resident models take 1.66 GB in about 10,800 files, more than all the rooms themselves | medium | v0.0.48-dev2 (2026_10_23_v0.0.48-dev2_dev2-try8_0754e9bc) | open | - | - | PERSISTING in v0.0.48-rc1, reopened (24 October 2026; the maintainer: "how is this even possible that the fucking models take MORE SPACE in the Quake crap than they did in Morrowind"). Measured on the rc1-try3 image: 4,225 complete actor models take 1,468 MB, of which 992 MB animation frames (64 per model, vertices median 1,461) and 587 MB skin textures (one 512 x 256 or 512 x 336 skin inside every model); the original Data Files are 1,840 MB and Morrowind.bsa 297 MB. Two design faults multiply it besides the missing composition (NPC-BAKED-WHOLE-DUPLICATION-33): each resident has a standing model and a separate mover model (the maintainer: "every NPC can be a mover model"), so every body is stored twice with two skins; and every model carries its own skin. Every character keeps all of its animation; the fix stores things once: one model per character, the modular NPC stages 2 and 3, skins stored once, an actor size budget (docs/MODULAR_NPCS.md "Where it stands", docs/ROADMAP.md). |

| ALIAS-BUDGET-32MB-48 | The alias model budget is still the 16 MB era's 512 KiB: two NPCs fall back to standing-only animations | medium | v0.0.48-dev2 | fixed | v0.0.48 | - | Fixed in v0.0.48 (branch v0.0.48-fix-content-32mb, 23 October 2026): the actors byte budget is sized for 32 MB of Fast RAM, 768 KiB (npc_anim.ACTOR_BYTE_BUDGET: room and town residents, the actor library), the engine direct alias stream limit (model.c AW_ALIAS_STREAM_LIMIT, the heap estimate alias_stream_heap.STREAM_LIMIT). The stream decodes a model straight into its Cache block, with no staging and no Hunk, so a model over the 512 KiB staging limit loads the same way on 16 MB, where the Cache evicts older models for it (native test: a 536 KB model streams with malloc failing, to the bytes the older Hunk path decodes). The malloc staging (AW_ALIAS_STAGING_LIMIT) and the player models keep 512 KiB. Measured on all 1,769 room residents and 415 actor library appearances (read only): under 512 KiB, 161 kits were over it at their first vertex rung and were baked at a lower one, and Neloth and Ienas Nadus fell back to their standing model; with 768 KiB none is reduced and none falls back (every kit fits: at most 666 faces x 64 samples, 720,032 bytes). Neloth and Ienas Nadus get the full react+full kit (movers 682,312 and 681,492 bytes, standing models 331,316 and 331,060). Cost: those standing models +12.6 KB on average (largest +89.6 KB, 2.2 MB in all), movers +3.8 MB in all; two movers at once are at most 1.44 MB of the Cache, within the 2 MiB Hunk gap of the 16 MB baseline, so no room or frame is marked for it. Not yet in a build or in game. |

| [BUILD-CHIM-STAIR-JOBS-AT-LAUNCH-42](bugs/BUILD-CHIM-STAIR-JOBS-AT-LAUNCH-42.md) | In-stage pools of the CHIM step stay at the --jobs the stage was launched with: the v0.0.42 final's stair gate walked on 2 workers with an allowance of 12 | high | v0.0.42 (2026_10_17_v0.0.42_release-try2_a241ba1) | fixed | v0.0.42 | - | 17 October 2026: fixed in source on the v0.0.42 line (v0.0.42-sched-memory): build_parallel.stage_ceiling (the build budget under the scheduler) sizes the stair gate, its walk units and the visibility rows; governed_map's memory governor plans for the pool's ceiling; the live allowance decides at each task. |

| BUILD-CHIM-VIS-RECOMPUTED-48 | The CHIM stage recomputed every region frame's visibility (1 h 20 min on 24 cores) | medium | v0.0.48-dev2 (2026_10_23_v0.0.48-dev2_dev2-try5_b4918af7) | fixed | v0.0.48 | - | Fixed for v0.0.48 on the branch v0.0.48-fix-reuse-keys (23 October 2026). Found: fire rows (chim.flames) are written after the visibility rows and key none of them; collision keys the chunk-visibility units only through the occluder grid (the solid columns of the placed collision pieces), which those units read, so a collision change that moves an occluder column recomputes the frames it is in, rightly (try 5 also rebuilt 9,434 variants). The over-keying was in the placement-visibility units, half of the 86,464: keyed by world-wide placement ids, which run on across frames, one placement more or less in an earlier frame renumbered every later frame and recomputed its units; they also counted chunk tops, the ring and every box of the frame, which they do not read. chim.world.chunk_visibility now keys and computes them in the frame's own numbering by what they read (ground, occluder grid, grain, frame corner, viewers, candidates, near sets, those candidates' boxes) and numbers the result back; units of the earlier key are found once, renumbered and stored in the new key, so the change of key costs no recompute. tests/test_chim_visibility_keys.py. The try 8 build reused every visibility unit (1 min 16 s). |

| [BUILD-IMAGE-UNDERUSED-32](bugs/BUILD-IMAGE-UNDERUSED-32.md) | Parts of the image step still run serially or leave most CPUs idle | medium | v0.0.32-dev | fixed | v0.0.48 | - | Fixed for v0.0.48 on the branch v0.0.48-fix-serial-steps: the payload preflight (run twice per image step; dev2 try 9: 45 s at 1.6 cores) and the room batch install. Read-only on the try 9 payload, 12 cores, the same report: 36 to 44 s -> 10.5 to 12.6 s. Engine map limits on the pool (12.5 to 15 s at 0.17 cores -> 2.0 to 2.2 s, also in finalize), the source manifest's sprite sections parsed by workers (10.4 s -> 2.1 s, the same sections), the layout walk by os.walk (about 4 s -> under 1 s), the Python-bound checks in processes of their own and the disk layout beside them, the room batch files hashed and copied on threads with a timed phase. Methods before behind AMIWIND_MAP_LIMITS_POOL=off, AMIWIND_JSON_POOL=ordered, AMIWIND_LAYOUT_WALK=pathlib, AMIWIND_PREFLIGHT_METHOD=threads, AMIWIND_ROOM_INSTALL_THREADS=1. tests/test_image_preflight_pool.py. Still on one core, each under a minute: the torch conversion (7.5 s) and the world UI (14.6 s, beside the combat data). |

| BUILD-MWSCRIPT-KEY-UNDERDECLARED-48 | The mwscript stage's reuse record is refused: the stage reads tools/mwscript/measure.py, which its fingerprint leaves out | medium | v0.0.48-dev2 (2026_10_23_v0.0.48-dev2_dev2-try3_d0241f9e) | fixed | v0.0.48 | - | Fixed for v0.0.48 on the branch v0.0.48-fix-reuse-keys (23 October 2026). Cause: prepare_mwscript.compiler_identity hashes the bytes of every tools/mwscript/*.py file (FOLDER.glob('*.py')), measure.py too, which the stage never imports; the key counted the files a folder holds only as data files other than Python files. Stage keys (form 3, tools/build_cache.py) now count the Python files such a glob reads, whole. The offline reuse audit of the try 2, 4, 5 and 7 traces finds no miss; tests/test_build_cache.py test_python_files_read_by_a_folder_glob_are_in_the_key, tests/test_stage_key_reach.py. The stage reruns once (seconds) with the new key. PERSISTING: seen again in the dev2 try 9 build (23 October 2026), whose release line 9ad40fdf does not carry this fix; the reuse audit flagged the stage as an unexpected rebuild (record refused). The rc1 line carries the fix. PERSISTING again in dev2 try 10 (23 October 2026, release line 96f4928a without the fix): record refused, unexpected rebuild. PERSISTING again in dev2 try 11 (23 October 2026, release line b8c6b6e9 without the fix). |

| BUILD-PLAN-BESIDE-STALE-48 | The resource plan held a reuse build at 1 of 24 room batch workers against memory that stages it no longer runs held in the measured run | high | v0.0.48-dev2 (2026_10_23_v0.0.48-dev2_dev2-try14_396a2dc2) | fixed | v0.0.48 | - | Fixed in v0.0.48 (23 October 2026): dev2 try 14 reran 4 of 42 stages; its start-up plan held chim-room-batches and the image at 1 of 24 workers, counting 43,386 and 26,736 MiB that other stages held beside them in try 13. The room batches ran the whole stage on 1.0 of 18.5 cores (4m47s, mostly cached units, so the cost was small); the runtime memory guard gave the image its workers back (15.0 of 24 cores on average). The memory measured beside a stage now counts only up to what the stages of THIS build that can run beside it hold at full speed: never the stages it waits for or that wait for it (from the scheduler table, build_parallel.stage_dependencies), and nothing when nothing else runs. Tests in tests/test_scheduler_memory_starved.py. |

| [BUILD-POOL-ARG-UNFINGERPRINTED-35](bugs/BUILD-POOL-ARG-UNFINGERPRINTED-35.md) | The balmora stage was never reusable once the shared storage pool grew past 512 MiB: its --npc-model-pool folder was hashed as an input | high | v0.0.35-dev1 (MiniWind v0.0.35-dev1 animkit-companion) | fixed | v0.0.35 | - | Fixed in v0.0.35 (7faafdb): the storage pool counts as a content-addressed token and pool reads must be keyed lookups; tests/test_build_pool_arg.py. |
| BUILD-POOL-MEMORY-PEAK-48 | The CHIM and room stages ran side by side at 42 of 44 GiB and were both throttled to one worker for over ten minutes | medium | v0.0.48-dev2 (2026_10_23_v0.0.48-dev2_dev2-try5_b4918af7) | fixed | v0.0.48 | - | Fixed for v0.0.48 on the branch v0.0.48-fix-build-speed. Cause confirmed: a pool's idle worker processes keep the memory they reached, so the scheduler's cut stopped new units but freed nothing and the memory never came back under the line. Each worker pool under the scheduler now ends its idle workers above the stage's allowance after about 20 s (build_parallel._watch_idle_workers; ordered maps of a stage pool through tools/pool_status.py); new ones start when the allowance grows. Measured: 8 idle workers 1,057 MiB -> 1 worker 132 MiB after a cut to 1 (tests/test_pool_idle_retire.py); room batches stage cut to 2 of 16 at 60 s: 14 idle workers ended, process memory 1.95 GiB -> 0.57 GiB during the cut, outputs byte-identical. Scheduling only: no stage key changes (checked over every tools script, base 178df662). Peaks apart: not scheduled apart; chim-room-batches is off the critical path, and with the workers ending a cut above the critical line frees memory, so the guard gives the share back instead of holding both stages at one worker. |
| BUILD-POOL-TINY-UNITS-48 | Stages hand their pools units too small to keep the cores busy (CHIM slices, the town stages) | medium | v0.0.48-dev2 (2026_10_23_v0.0.48-dev2_dev2-try5_b4918af7) | fixed | v0.0.48 | - | Fixed for v0.0.48 on the branch v0.0.48-fix-build-speed. completed_map hands out guided chunks of small units (one task each, at most about 2 s of measured work; a chunk is pickled once, so the heap gate's slices of a frame send their arrays once); AMIWIND_POOL_CHUNKS=off keeps the old method. On the dev2 world (16 cores): heap gate 14.9 s -> 13.8 s, zone walk 65.3 s -> 61.3 s, reports byte-identical. The chim-town stages read only their own frame (chim.frame_map.frame_alone through chim.validate.load_frame) instead of the whole world: 1-3 s instead of 35-139 s, the same frame row and models (SHA-256); the balmora town stage reached the same point 153 s -> 22 s with the same 45 files. The whole-world read decodes each terrain record once, on the workers (separate commit; keys the chim stage): 139 s on 1 core -> 32 s, the same world (SHA-256). Not covered: maps on ordered_map (its text keys every stage), such as the validation's per-frame cross-checks. |

| BUILD-REUSE-OUTPUTS-DIFFER-48 | Eight stages were rebuilt because their earlier outputs differed from their records (character, reading, world-ui, actor-contact, world-terrain and others) | medium | v0.0.48-dev2 (2026_10_23_v0.0.48-dev2_dev2-try5_b4918af7) | fixed | v0.0.48 | - | Fixed for v0.0.48 on the branch v0.0.48-fix-reuse-keys (23 October 2026). Cause: all seven stages were refused for one earlier stage, door-audio ('an earlier stage it depends on ran again and its outputs differ: door-audio'). door-audio first ran in try 4, after intro, census, area and balmora-interiors had been reused as read-only links to the storage pool; as an in-place scene stage it first made every one of those files private and writable (mwad.paths.unshare_tree, BUILD-POOL-READONLY-SCENE-WRITE-35: same bytes, new inode), and the stage record took all 2,124 files of intro-scene as its outputs. In try 5 area and balmora-interiors ran (writable files), door-audio wrote its 18 files, and the comparison with the try 4 record failed. The same over-long record (door-audio claimed intro-scene/id1/maps/seyda.bsp, which it never writes) also kept opening-references and door-audio itself from reuse in try 5. Now the record leaves out every file a stage only unshared: before it ran, a read-only hard link to the pool object named by the SHA-256 the file still has, same size, mode changed at most in its write bits (Recorder.unshared; the record counts them as 'unshared'). tests/test_build_cache.py test_unshared_pool_links_are_not_outputs_of_an_in_place_stage. Records written before the fix are not rewritten; the next builds reuse from try 5 or later, whose door-audio record holds its 18 files. PERSISTING: seen again in the dev2 try 9 build (23 October 2026), on the release line 9ad40fdf that does not carry the fix; the rc1 line carries it. (try 9: chim-interiors rebuilt, outputs differ.) |
| BUILD-REUSE-RC1-RECORDS-REFUSED-48 | Every stage that ran the idle-worker watcher or read the CHIM label module was marked not reusable: rc1-try2 had 24 unexpected rebuilds | high | v0.0.48-rc1 (2026_10_24_v0.0.48-rc1_rc1-try2_e341aa33) | fixed | v0.0.48 | - | Fixed in v0.0.48 (24 October 2026): the v0.0.48-rc1 build marked its stages not reusable at their end ("ran 1 repository functions its fingerprint left out (first: tools/build_parallel.py:_watch_stage_pool)"; five also "read 1 repository files its fingerprint left out (first: tools/chim_compat.py)"), so the rc1-try2 reuse preflight planned 24 UNEXPECTED rebuilds and was stopped after 6 minutes. Both are output-neutral by design: _watch_stage_pool is a scheduling unit (SCHEDULING_UNITS, BUILD-POOL-MEMORY-PEAK-48) started by the pool_status instrumentation hook, which the reach does not follow; tools/chim_compat.py is loaded only by the cache key code (BUILD-CHIM-LABEL-IN-KEYS-48). check_calls now skips scheduling units and chim_compat.py is an instrumentation module. The rc1 records stay refused (the verdict is stored at stage end), so rc1-try3 rebuilds those stages once; the final reuses them. |

| BUILD-ROOM-BATCHES-KEY-UNDERDECLARED-48 | The room stage's reuse record is refused: it reads 17 repository files its fingerprint leaves out | medium | v0.0.48-dev2 (2026_10_23_v0.0.48-dev2_dev2-try6_4b9ea2b3) | fixed | v0.0.48 | - | Fixed for v0.0.48 on the branch v0.0.48-fix-reuse-keys (23 October 2026). Cause: the room stage keys its resident bakes with UnitCache.open('resident-bake', {}, __file__) in tools/prepare_area.py (resident_unit); that key hashes prepare_area.py's whole script reach (pass_cache.source_digest and environment), 17 files the room stage's own reach never had (config/surface-flattening.json, config/terrain-visual-cull.json, config/visual-offsets.json, tools/chim/seyda.py, collision_bsp, compact_bsp, exterior_plan, map_files, planar_merge, prepare_world_scenery, routed_hull, surface_flatten, terrain_visual_cull, visual_offsets, visual_primitives, world_payload, world_scenery). Stage keys (form 3) now follow the code any reached call hands to a cache key (PassCache.open, UnitCache.open, source_digest: a module, module.__file__ or (module, 'function')) with the reach that key reads, whole files, and its AMIWIND_* names. The offline reuse audit of the try 5 and 7 traces finds no miss; tests/test_build_cache.py test_code_handed_to_a_cache_key_is_in_the_key, tests/test_stage_key_reach.py. The stage reruns once with the new key (its rooms and residents come from the unit cache). PERSISTING: seen again in the dev2 try 9 build (23 October 2026), whose release line 9ad40fdf does not carry this fix; the reuse audit flagged the stage as an unexpected rebuild (record refused). The rc1 line carries the fix. PERSISTING again in dev2 try 10 (23 October 2026, release line 96f4928a without the fix): record refused, unexpected rebuild. |

| BUILD-SEYDA-BSP-CARRIER-48 | Stages pass data through a legacy map file: seyda.bsp's entities and compiled room.bsp files carry what JSON could | medium | v0.0.48-dev2 | fixed | v0.0.48 | - | 23 October 2026: found while fixing BUILD-ROOM-BATCHES-SCENE-RACE-48 (the maintainer: 'we are not using antiquated crap like that anymore'). Five stages wrote into seyda.bsp's entity lump, nine read only the hand timings from it, and the town room stages compiled each room (qbsp, vis, light) to keep only its entities and a few boxes. Fixed in source for v0.0.48: (a) the eight stage readers (chim_room_batches, prepare_area, chim_town, chim_rooms, prepare_census, prepare_balmora_interiors, prepare_interior, import_town) take the hand timings and eye height from the hands stage's report (hand_metadata.scene_timings: scene-ready.json 'hands', else hands-report.json; the same lines byte for byte, so map bytes and unit keys do not change for that reason); the legacy region rebuild (rebuild_balmora_region) keeps reading the region map it rebuilds. chim-room-batches waits for the census alone again (tools/build_parallel.py). (c) In a build with CHIM rooms the town room stages (area, balmora-interiors, chim-rooms-TOWN) write shell rooms (--room-form shell, prepare_area.shell_room): no qbsp, vis or light; placements.json holds what the compiled room map placed (prepare_area.mesh_placements, the mesh converter's rule: the same set for all 197 town rooms of the v0.0.48-dev2 try5 run); a room with an object the engine activates is still compiled; --interior-format bsp compiles every room as before. Measured like for like (the same 197 rooms, 16 workers, no unit cache, busy host): 599 s wall and 7,033 s CPU compiled, 148 s and 1,781 s as shells (area 45 to 20 s, balmora-interiors 157 to 32 s, chim-rooms-vivec_city 397 to 96 s). (b) moved to its own entry, BUILD-SEYDA-ENTITY-BUS-48. Tests: tests/test_hand_metadata.py, tests/test_room_shell.py, tests/test_room_batches.py. |

| [BUILD-SEYDA-ENTITY-BUS-48](bugs/BUILD-SEYDA-ENTITY-BUS-48.md) | Four scene stages still pass Seyda Neen's entities through the seyda.bsp carrier's entity lump | medium | v0.0.48-dev2 | fixed | v0.0.48 | - | 23 October 2026: part (b) of BUILD-SEYDA-BSP-CARRIER-48. The scene chain added Seyda Neen's entities through the seyda.bsp carrier's entity lump and chim-town-seyda parsed the whole .bsp. Fixed in source for v0.0.48: Seyda Neen's scene entity list SCENE/seyda-entities.json (tools/seyda_entities.py: the entity text byte for byte, the brush model boxes, the lump sizes, the SHA-256 of the seyda.bsp beside it) is published by every stage that writes the entities (bsp carrier and compiled, the standalone carrier tool, npcs, hands, intro, area, opening-references); the writers start from the list's text, not the lump, and refuse a list that does not belong to the map they rewrite; chim-town-seyda reads only the list, no .bsp (chim_town.seyda_maps_from, source_carrier_check). The legacy read path stays behind --seyda-entities bsp (AMIWIND_SEYDA_ENTITIES, part of the stage keys; the default removes the variable). Outputs byte-identical: tests/test_seyda_entities.py runs the writers and the reader both ways on a carrier scene and compares every file. chim-room-batches already waited for census alone; chim-town-seyda still waits for opening-references, which writes the final list. Left for v0.0.49: seyda.bsp is still written beside the list (non-native image, actor check, compiled source, and the chim-town record names its SHA-256); dropping it changes that record. |

| [BUILD-STAGE-INTERRUPT-ABSORBED-36](bugs/BUILD-STAGE-INTERRUPT-ABSORBED-36.md) | The stage wrapper absorbed Ctrl-C: a stage that handled the interrupt and returned 0 counted as passed, and the build went on | high | v0.0.36-dev4 | fixed | v0.0.36-dev5 | - | fixed in v0.0.36-dev5 (9ec4893, in build-timeline d4a0ff0): the stage wrapper hands Ctrl-C on and the build ends by it (tests/test_build_profile.py); gate 1878 and integration gate 2025 passed; published on the integration line (round 16, 0587c38) |
| BUILD-STAGE-KEY-WHOLE-CLASS-48 | A one-method edit in tools/actor_grounding.py rekeyed the CHIM stages: a class was import-time code of its module, so every stage importing the module hashed every method | medium | v0.0.48-dev2 (2026_10_23_v0.0.48-dev2_dev2-try8_0754e9bc) | fixed | v0.0.48 | - | Fixed for v0.0.48 on the branch v0.0.48-fix-reuse-keys (23 October 2026). Cause: commit 62827968 (the ground bake, ACTOR-GROUND-BAKE-CHIM-ROOMS-48) changed one method, actor_grounding._Fitter._scene; scope 'units' counted classes as their module's import-time code, so chim, chim-rooms-vivec_city and chim-room-batches, which import the module for initial_state and never run the fitter, were rekeyed ('input change: sources (tools/actor_grounding.py)') and try 8 reran them. Stage keys now use form 3: a plain class (no decorator, metaclass or repository base class; methods and literal attributes only) is a unit of its own, its body and each method ('Class.method'); a bare use reaches every method, Class.static_method() only that one; the read trace names methods as Class.method and checks them. chim and chim-rooms-vivec_city no longer reach _Fitter. chim-room-batches still rekeys for that edit, and must: its resident bake key (UnitCache.open with prepare_area.py, BUILD-ROOM-BATCHES-KEY-UNDERDECLARED-48) hashes prepare_area.py's whole reach, actor_grounding.py included, so the bakes it reuses change with it; narrowing that key to the bake worker would rebake all 1,769 residents once (31 min), left for a later release. A reuse source keyed in form 2 is compared in form 2 (the change of form rebuilds nothing; predicted against try 8: every stage but engine keeps its key). tests/test_build_cache.py test_a_method_a_stage_never_reaches_keeps_its_key, test_stage_keys_of_the_older_form_are_compared_in_that_form, test_a_reuse_source_in_the_old_key_form_is_reused_whole; tests/test_stage_key_reach.py. |

| [BUILD-STATS-SERIAL-36](bugs/BUILD-STATS-SERIAL-36.md) | The CHIM stats step runs serially and silently on one core | medium | v0.0.36-dev3 | fixed | v0.0.48 | - | Fixed for v0.0.48 on the branch v0.0.48-fix-serial-steps. The stats no longer parse the 694 MB source manifest again (chim_build hands over each frame's area and cell: 10.4 s on one core measured on the try 9 manifest, 16 to 18.5 s in loaded builds); the models' faces are made once on the pool for the stats and the cameras (the cameras' report decoded all 32,714 models again on one core); the pool maps use build_parallel.in_order_map (ordered_map with a cost counted every held unit before each submission: 10 million done() checks in the parent); ring offsets once per grain, distributions with one sort, every section a timed phase. Synthetic dev2-size world on 8 cores: 13.4 s -> 10.1 s (JSON writing 5.1 s -> 2.2 s), the same bytes; the dev2 step (44.0 s at 1.4 cores in try 8) is expected at about 15 to 20 s. AMIWIND_STATS_POOL=off and AMIWIND_JSON_POOL=ordered keep the method before. tests/test_stats_pool.py. |

Tests: tests/test_interior_counts.py. |

| [CHIM-32MB-SAVE-ENTRY-48](bugs/CHIM-32MB-SAVE-ENTRY-48.md) | A save, a start or a console jump into a place marked for 32 MB of Fast RAM was not refused on 16 MB | high | v0.0.48-dev2 | fixed | v0.0.48 | - | Fixed in v0.0.48 (branch v0.0.48-fix-32mb-saves, 23 October 2026): every entry into a place goes through one check, AW_SceneEntryRefuse (aw_scene.c): the room mark or frame mark of the target map, and for a save also the frame holding the saved position. Loading a save (Load game, quick load, the death screen), a quick or direct start, dbg tp / aw_scene / the camera marks / the strider / a door out of a room, and a raw map, changelevel or Quake load are refused on less Fast RAM (16 MB found, or Extra RAM off) before anything is left or loaded, with the refused-room screen (interior / area). ENTER returns to the main menu for a load or a start, to the game where the player stood for a console jump during play. Engine tests (aga_scene_test.c, aga_chim_save_test.c, test_chim_room_fast_ram.py); not yet in a build or in game. |

| CHIM-FRAME-16MB-REFUSAL-48 | Five CHIM region frames are over or within 64 KiB of the 16 MB heap budget: on 16 MB of Fast RAM (or with Extra RAM off) their rings may not fit the zone | high | v0.0.48-dev2 (2026_10_23_v0.0.48-dev2_dev2-try8_0754e9bc) | fixed | v0.0.48 | - | Fixed in v0.0.48 (branch v0.0.48-fix-content-32mb, 23 October 2026), by the maintainer rule for this release: a place over the budget is built at full quality and marked as needing 32 MB of Fast RAM, as the rooms are (CHIM-ROOM-16MB-REFUSAL-48). The CHIM heap gate (tools/chim/heap.py mark_frames) gives each frame fast_mb: a frame whose gated ring is over the 16 MB budget or within 64 KiB of it (FRAME_NEAR_BYTES, about 1 % of the 6,242,304-byte budget) and fits what the 32 MB minimum gives it (the same zone and the automatic 8 MiB CHIM cache bank for its models and textures) is built as is and marked; one that fits neither still fails the gate, as does a frame over the efrag links. The stage writes the counts into chim-receipt.json (fast_ram_32mb: frames, over_16mb, frame_records, near_bytes, bank_bytes) for the build summary (BUILD-SUMMARY-INTERIOR-COUNTS-48), and the image step writes _chim_frame_fast_mb 32 first in each marked frame map worldspawn. At the crossing into a marked frame on less Fast RAM (16 MB found, or Extra RAM off) the engine keeps the player at the last spot inside the frame he stands in and loads nothing of it; the first time the refused-room screen on the loading art says: Sorry, but this area cannot be run with 16MB of Fast RAM. Press ENTER to return; then the message box every 3 seconds (aw_scene.c AW_SceneFrameRefuse). Measured on the v0.0.48-dev2 builds (1,351 frames, read only): 5 frames marked, none over the budget: x-07 y-04 (1,296 bytes to spare), x-08 y-04 (4,128), x-11 y+15 (18,928), x+09 y+02 (20,048), x+17 y+04 (35,968); the next frame has 179,136. Not yet in a build or in game; a start or a saved game inside a marked frame is not refused. |

| [CHIM-ROOM-OVER-BUDGET-RETRY-48](bugs/CHIM-ROOM-OVER-BUDGET-RETRY-48.md) | A model over the image budget is tried whole again on every run before its cached tiles are used: its room runs on one core | low | v0.0.48-dev2 | fixed | v0.0.48 | - | Fixed for v0.0.48 on the branch v0.0.48-fix-serial-steps. Cause: a unit over the image budget raised, so nothing was stored under its key and every run compiled the model whole again (twice with the serial rerun). The refusal is now the unit's result (SoftError) and is cached under its key; a later run takes the verdict from the cache and goes to the cached tiles. A run with the method before (AMIWIND_BUDGET_RERUN=serial) counts a cached verdict as a miss. tests/test_budget_tiles.py: the second run calls no worker. |

| [ENGINE-HEAP-FIXED-SIZE-43](bugs/ENGINE-HEAP-FIXED-SIZE-43.md) | The game heap is a fixed 11 MiB from the build: with 128 MB of RAM Vivec still runs under the 2 MiB safety gap and loads pause | medium | v0.0.43-dev2 (2026_10_18_v0.0.43-dev2_full-batch3_b55a5ce) | fixed | v0.0.45 | - | Fixed in v0.0.45 (branch v0.0.43-prefetch, merged in the v0.0.45 line): the heap is sized at start from the largest free Fast RAM block less CHIM margin (2 MiB + an eighth), grown by a quarter of the room beyond the built 11 MiB when that room holds it plus 8 MiB, at most 24 MiB; -heapmb as asked, aw_autoram 0 keeps the built size. FS-UAE, dev2 data, 128 MiB: a 24 MiB heap and an 82 MiB CHIM cache; no hunk-gap safety warning in 5 laps of Balmora, Caldera, Vivec and Seyda Neen (dev2 as built: 4 warnings on one open-world route); 16 MiB keeps 11 MiB. |

| PREFLIGHT-GUARD-UNSTAMPED-NPCS-48 | The payload preflight guard count refused census.bsp: its three NPCs get their source identity later in the image step | critical | v0.0.48-rc1 (2026_10_23_v0.0.48-rc1_rc1_18a24ead) | fixed | v0.0.48 | - | Fixed in v0.0.48 (24 October 2026): the v0.0.48-rc1 build stopped in the image step payload preflight after 1 h 30 min: "guard-records: Staged NPC lacks original source identity: census.bsp". The early guard torch count (added for GUARD-TORCH-TYPES-LIMIT-48) read the staged scene maps before the image step stamps their NPCs; census.bsp (Socucius Ergalla 119636, Sellus Gravius 172858, Ganciele Douar 172857) has no aw_source_id until then (dev2 try 14 image copy: stamped). The early count now lists NPCs with no identity yet and skips them (a lower bound, as documented); an identity that names no record is still refused early, and the image step guard torches refuse an unstamped NPC on the final maps. Checked on the rc1 run: 25 records, 3 unstamped census NPCs, no error. |

| [RAM-REQUIREMENT-32MB-48](bugs/RAM-REQUIREMENT-32MB-48.md) | 16 MB of Fast RAM no longer holds the whole game: 32 MB is the safe minimum, 16 MB starts with a warning | medium | v0.0.48-dev2 | fixed | v0.0.48-dev2 | - | Fixed in v0.0.48-dev2 (branch v0.0.48-fast-ram-32), decided for the project 23 October 2026: for now the game requires 32 MB of Fast RAM, the safe minimum, which should be enough for the entire game; more RAM is used automatically (heap 13 MiB at 32 MB, 20 at 64, 24 at 128, unchanged). Below 32 MB the boot check shows the Fast RAM row as [!] WARN and, under the checklist's closing line, the note 'NOTE: AmiWind has grown since v0.0.48: 32 MB of Fast RAM is now the minimum. Only N MB Fast RAM found, hence the game will start but may load more often. Press ENTER to continue anyway.' with a ten-second countdown under it (ENTER skips; it never blocks, so unattended 16 MB runs go on); the engine repeats the note without a wait on the CLI, the console and the startup logo. The launcher, the playtest packages' emulator configs and the shipped play presets start with 32 MB; 16 MB stays a choice with a warning. Found 22-23 October 2026 with every outdoor NPC and creature placed (781 actors in 336 CHIM region frames): 14 frames need more model cache than the 16 MB baseline gives (about 2.1 MB; worst rcp2_m7 over by 2.0 MB); actor models per frame 624 KB median, 4.13 MB max; with a 24 MiB heap (about 15.7 MB of cache) every frame fits; interiors: largest Balmora room set 4.10 MB of a 7.03 MB room zone; on 16 MB the engine evicts and reloads models: slower, not a crash. |

| [ROOM-ACTORS-FLOATING-48](bugs/ROOM-ACTORS-FLOATING-48.md) | Regression: 1,662 residents of batch CHIM rooms stood above the floor, never fitted to it | critical | v0.0.48-dev2 (2026_10_23_v0.0.48-dev2_dev2-try11_b8c6b6e9) | fixed | v0.0.48 | - | REGRESSION of ACTOR-GROUND-BAKE-CHIM-ROOMS-48. Fixed for v0.0.48 on branch v0.0.48-fix-room-grounding-chim (23 October 2026): tools/room_actor_bake.py fits every CHIM room resident in the image step on the room's own collision (the first walkable surface within 8 over and 32 under, origin 0.25 over it, every sole within -0.5..1.0 on the whole room collision, else the nearest spot within 48), and require_fitted stops the image step if any scene map's ground resident was never fitted. Not a scale or coordinate error: origins equal the game data times 0.25 exactly; store and classic floors equal (0.000 over 450 whole-room residents); headroom does not grow with height (slope -0.0003). On a read-only copy of all 1,132 rooms of try 11: of the 1,662, 1,643 grounded, 19 kept as reasoned explicit exceptions (ROOM-RESIDENTS-NO-FIT-48). Not yet seen in a build. |

| ROOM-RESIDENTS-KEY-SERIAL-49 | The room residents step opens its unit cache once per resident: 233 s on one core before any cached resident is restored | medium | v0.0.48-dev2 | fixed | v0.0.48 | - | Fixed for v0.0.48 on the branch v0.0.48-fix-build-speed: the residents step (prepare_area.populate: room batches, town rooms, area) opens the resident-bake unit cache and reads the game data identity once per stage (prepare_area.open_residents; per resident that was about 47 ms for the cache's environment and 40 ms for the 7 MB input lock, on one core), hashes the level list once, and restores on the stage's threads. The keys are the same whichever way the cache is opened (tests/test_resident_restore_once.py). Measured 23 October 2026, room batches 1-8 on 16 cores, warm caches: the residents phase 43.1 s -> 9.0 s (207 residents looked up in 1.8 s), the stage 66.0 s -> 31.0 s; every output byte-identical (SHA-256) except the timing fields. The code change keys the resident bakes and room units once (prepare_area.py is in their code key). PERSISTING: seen again in the dev2 try 9 build (23 October 2026), on the release line 9ad40fdf that does not carry the fix; the rc1 line carries it. (try 9: chim-room-batches 3 min 53 s at 1.02 of 18 cores.) |

| [BUILD-ACTOR-MODEL-UNSUPPORTED-48](bugs/BUILD-ACTOR-MODEL-UNSUPPORTED-48.md) | A Balmora build-speed measurement stopped on Unsupported actor model | medium | v0.0.48-dev2 | closed | - | - | Closed 23 October 2026, not reproducible on the v0.0.48 release line (7f5d043e). The measurement's scratch run was deleted and no log on the build volume carries the message (every 22-23 October v0.0.48 run on ws8 searched read only). With the head's decoder, 6,698 models decode or refuse for another reason: the dev2 try9 actor library (5,173, all decode) and the Balmora rooms CHIM image (1,525: 1,337 decode, 188 resident copies with a shared-skin record refuse as grouped skin); none is refused as unsupported. The head's writers stay inside the decoder's limits (at most 666 faces = 1,998 vertices and 256 frames, ALIAS-BUDGET-32MB-48). Most likely a model read from the work-in-progress run's partly assembled id1; not provable without the log. The unnamed message is ACTOR-MODEL-ERROR-UNNAMED-48. |

### Tagged performance

- [AW-20260928-01](bugs/AW-20260928-01.md): Prison ship to deck transition is intermittently very slow or freezes (FS-UAE) (open)
- [BENCH-DISK-BYTES-32](bugs/BENCH-DISK-BYTES-32.md): FS-UAE moves disk bytes at about 90 MB/s, so it cannot price what a walk reads (open)
- [BENCH-HOST-STORAGE-32](bugs/BENCH-HOST-STORAGE-32.md): Emulator disk timings with the hard file in a Windows folder measure the PC, not FFS (open)
- [BENCH-SESSION-DRIFT-32](bugs/BENCH-SESSION-DRIFT-32.md): Emulated disk time drifts about 1.6x between FS-UAE sessions (open)
- [BUILD-BODY-BAKE-SLOWER-44](bugs/BUILD-BODY-BAKE-SLOWER-44.md): The image step's player body bake ran 42 % slower than its baseline with the same 40 bodies (open)
- [BUILD-CACHE-NO-CUTOFF-33](bugs/BUILD-CACHE-NO-CUTOFF-33.md): A rebuilt stage whose outputs come out unchanged still rebuilds every stage after it (open)
- [BUILD-CACHE-OWNER-FAILS-STAGE-34](bugs/BUILD-CACHE-OWNER-FAILS-STAGE-34.md): A cache entry owned by another user failed a release build four minutes in, instead of being rebuilt or caught before any stage (open)
- [BUILD-CHIM-MAIN-MEMORY-46](bugs/BUILD-CHIM-MAIN-MEMORY-46.md): The CHIM stage main process holds so much memory that the memory guard starves its own workers (open)
- [BUILD-CHIM-REGION-FRAMES-TAIL-43](bugs/BUILD-CHIM-REGION-FRAMES-TAIL-43.md): The CHIM region-frame step runs its last frames on one worker while the other cores sit idle (open)
- [BUILD-CHIM-SOURCE-REPEAT-41](bugs/BUILD-CHIM-SOURCE-REPEAT-41.md): The CHIM stage parsed the whole master and read the same models again for every area: 723 source stages took 24,500 CPU seconds (open)
- [BUILD-CHIM-STAGE-IDLE-START-36](bugs/BUILD-CHIM-STAGE-IDLE-START-36.md): The chim stage of the 270-frame coast build spends its first 35-45 minutes on 1-4 cores while it holds 12-24 workers (open)
- [BUILD-CHIM-STAGE-SERIAL-TAIL-36](bugs/BUILD-CHIM-STAGE-SERIAL-TAIL-36.md): After the parallel world build the chim stage ran its validation, heap gate and zone walk on one core (about 10 minutes of the 270-frame coast build) (open)
- [BUILD-CHIM-VIS-ROWS-SERIAL-42](bugs/BUILD-CHIM-VIS-ROWS-SERIAL-42.md): The CHIM visibility rows of a build with many new frames run on one worker at a time and print nothing for half an hour or more (open)
- [BUILD-CHIM-WORLD-SERIAL-TAIL-41](bugs/BUILD-CHIM-WORLD-SERIAL-TAIL-41.md): After the region frames the CHIM world conversion prepared the towns one after another and worked out 723 frames' visibility rows in turn: about 7 minutes on 1 to 5 of 24 cores (open)
- [BUILD-ENGINE-KEY-SDK-33](bugs/BUILD-ENGINE-KEY-SDK-33.md): The engine stage is never reused: the Amiga SDK is not part of its key (open)
- [BUILD-ENGINE-SOURCE-KEY-BROAD-36](bugs/BUILD-ENGINE-SOURCE-KEY-BROAD-36.md): Any engine source edit changes the keys of the chim and world-flora stages (open)
- [BUILD-HEAP-GATE-AFTER-STAIRS-46](bugs/BUILD-HEAP-GATE-AFTER-STAIRS-46.md): The 12-second CHIM heap gate runs after the two-hour stair walk, so an over-budget frame fails the build late (open)
- [BUILD-IDLE-STAGES-33](bugs/BUILD-IDLE-STAGES-33.md): Scene-chain stages leave most of the workers they hold idle (open)
- [BUILD-IMAGE-FRAME-MAP-HEAP-SERIAL-36](bugs/BUILD-IMAGE-FRAME-MAP-HEAP-SERIAL-36.md): The image step's CHIM frame-map heap check runs on one core: 66 s to 4.5 min per build while 23 cores idle (open)
- [BUILD-IMAGE-NO-RESUME-33](bugs/BUILD-IMAGE-NO-RESUME-33.md): A late failure in the image step reruns the whole image step, and the failing check could have run in its first seconds (open)
- [BUILD-IMAGE-NOT-INCREMENTAL-33](bugs/BUILD-IMAGE-NOT-INCREMENTAL-33.md): The image step redoes every per-map pass and the whole pack on every build (open)
- [BUILD-IMAGE-REGION-MAPS-SERIAL-43](bugs/BUILD-IMAGE-REGION-MAPS-SERIAL-43.md): The image step's CHIM frame-map phases run on one core: the world read six times, ground traces on whole frames (open)
- [BUILD-IMAGE-SOURCE-MANIFEST-REPARSED-47](bugs/BUILD-IMAGE-SOURCE-MANIFEST-REPARSED-47.md): The image step parses the 694 MB CHIM source manifest three times, on one core each (16.0 s, 18.5 s, 17.1 s) (open)
- [BUILD-IMAGE-STAIR-WALK-CORES-43](bugs/BUILD-IMAGE-STAIR-WALK-CORES-43.md): The image step's stair walk used 45 % of its cores in the v0.0.43-dev3 build (95 % before): the perf guard failed its walk_unit section (open)
- [BUILD-IMAGE-WORLD-READ-FRAMES-47](bugs/BUILD-IMAGE-WORLD-READ-FRAMES-47.md): The image step CHIM world read ran its frame-read pool at 2.96 of 24 cores for 102 s (91 units): perf guard FIX-NOW (open)
- [BUILD-PARTITION-GROWTH-UNGATED-36](bugs/BUILD-PARTITION-GROWTH-UNGATED-36.md): Nothing gates the number of world partitions or the growth of the image (open)
- [BUILD-PASS-KEY-TOP-BROAD-43](bugs/BUILD-PASS-KEY-TOP-BROAD-43.md): A pass key counts a reached module's whole import-time code: a new constant in chim/models.py rekeyed all 1,295 region frame maps (open)
- [BUILD-PIPELINE-UNDERUTILIZATION-36](bugs/BUILD-PIPELINE-UNDERUTILIZATION-36.md): Umbrella: several builder phases run on one core while the rest of the machine idles, and some do work the build no longer needs (open)
- [BUILD-REUSE-APPLY-SERIAL-41](bugs/BUILD-REUSE-APPLY-SERIAL-41.md): A reused stage staged its files one at a time: world terrain, scenery and flora took 8 to 12 seconds each, one after another, at the start of every reuse build (open)
- [BUILD-REUSE-KEYS-TOO-BROAD-35](bugs/BUILD-REUSE-KEYS-TOO-BROAD-35.md): A whole build reused no stage, and the cost was not visible before the build started (open)
- [BUILD-REUSE-VERIFY-DISK-48](bugs/BUILD-REUSE-VERIFY-DISK-48.md): Reusing the chim stage reads and hashes all 9.35 GB of its outputs again: 78 s at 1.7 of 11 cores, counted as an underused stage (open)
- [BUILD-SCHEDULER-EVEN-SHARE-33](bugs/BUILD-SCHEDULER-EVEN-SHARE-33.md): The stage scheduler splits the workers evenly between running stages, so the longest stage gets no more than the shortest (MiniWind media on 1 of 4 workers for 1,195 s) (open)
- [BUILD-SILENT-PHASES-36](bugs/BUILD-SILENT-PHASES-36.md): Builder phases run silent: no start/end timing, no progress, no heartbeat; the CHIM stats step is a single-core tail (open)
- [BUILD-STAGE-ALLOWANCE-STUCK-43](bugs/BUILD-STAGE-ALLOWANCE-STUCK-43.md): A build stage's worker allowance stays at 1 after the other stages finish, so the CHIM stage runs on one core (open)
- [BUILD-STAGE-START-SHARE-33](bugs/BUILD-STAGE-START-SHARE-33.md): A pooled stage that starts while others run gets one worker as its start value (open)
- [BUILD-STAIR-KEY-BOOKKEEPING-36](bugs/BUILD-STAIR-KEY-BOOKKEEPING-36.md): Stair walk keys count the progress lines (stair_progress.py) and the record bookkeeping (stair_records.py): an edit to either rekeys every walk (open)
- [BUILD-STAIR-WALK-BORDER-COPIES-41](bugs/BUILD-STAIR-WALK-BORDER-COPIES-41.md): The CHIM stair gate walks every frame's whole border, so each cell's flights are walked many times and unreachable copies can fail a build (open)
- [BUILD-STAIR-WALK-SCOPE-KEY-46](bugs/BUILD-STAIR-WALK-SCOPE-KEY-46.md): Development builds walk every step of the island again after any collision change: the CHIM gate ignored the walk scope and keyed each frame by all of its collision (open)
- [BUILD-STORAGE-DUPLICATES-33](bugs/BUILD-STORAGE-DUPLICATES-33.md): Build storage keeps full duplicate copies of images, stage outputs and packages, and nothing removes them (open)
- [CHIM-CROSSING-SYNC-LOAD-43](bugs/CHIM-CROSSING-SYNC-LOAD-43.md): Every open-world cell crossing re-reads the world index and every earlier frame header in the main loop, then primes the ring with hundreds of small reads: 0.3-1.1 s pauses (open)
- [CHIM-HIDDEN-FACES-33](bugs/CHIM-HIDDEN-FACES-33.md): CHIM draws placed-model faces under the terrain that the recorded Seyda Neen region maps cull (open)
- [CHIM-HULL-CHAIN-COST-33](bugs/CHIM-HULL-CHAIN-COST-33.md): Large placed models collide through one long chain of convex pieces: a trace near the Arena canton walks about 6,600 planes (open)
- [CHIM-LEAF-SPAN-33](bugs/CHIM-LEAF-SPAN-33.md): 160 of 1,488 Balmora placements span more than 16 leaves (open)
- [CHIM-SEYDA-HUNK-GAP-33](bugs/CHIM-SEYDA-HUNK-GAP-33.md): Seyda Neen's CHIM map leaves less than the 2 MiB Hunk-gap safety at the default zone (open)
- [CHIM-STAIRGATE-SLOW-33](bugs/CHIM-STAIRGATE-SLOW-33.md): The CHIM stair gate takes over 10 minutes on the Balmora and Vivec Arena world (open)
- [CHIM-VIEW-FACES-33](bugs/CHIM-VIEW-FACES-33.md): Model faces dominate each Balmora view: the streamer alone does not fix frame rate (open)
- [CHIM-ZONE-BUDGET-33](bugs/CHIM-ZONE-BUDGET-33.md): The engine's default CHIM zone does not hold the active ring of Seyda Neen or of Balmora's south-west corner (open)

- [COLLISION-HULL-CHAINS-33](bugs/COLLISION-HULL-CHAINS-33.md): 183 brush models across the island collide through standing-hull chains 512 to 31,659 clipnodes deep (open)
- [COLLISION-TRACE-COST-33](bugs/COLLISION-TRACE-COST-33.md): Movement traces near large models walk long standing-hull chains: tens of ms per trace on a slow 68040 (emulator-relative) (open)

- [REMOTE-CONSOLE-LOG-COST-32](bugs/REMOTE-CONSOLE-LOG-COST-32.md): With the remote console on, every console line costs about 7-18 ms in FS-UAE (open)
